Theorem 218.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Every convergence analysis deformation of the grammars object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 476. The moduli space has dimension at most 773 with 1 connected components.

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to grammars theory, we decompose the problem into 476 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 773. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(1/n). QED.

Each subproblem has a solution by the existence and uniqueness fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 218.1001 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The grammars variety V defined by convergence analysis constraints admits a stratification into 978 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 308 and intersection form of rank 1.

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_978(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 978. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^308(S, F) is trivial. QED.
